\displaystyle\leq\|(\mathcal{I}_{G}+\mathcal{K}_{G})^{-1}\|_{0}\|\mathcal{L}\|_{0}\lesssim\tilde{\zeta}_{s}^{\alpha_{0}}\delta_{s}^{-\frac{31}{15}},

(4.131)

and for
\alpha\in(0,\alpha_{1}]
,

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{N}\|_{\alpha}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\|(\mathcal{I}_{G}+\mathcal{K}_{G})^{-1}\|_{\alpha}\|\mathcal{K}\|_{0}+\|(\mathcal{I}_{G}+\mathcal{K}_{G})^{-1}\|_{0}\|\mathcal{K}\|_{\alpha}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\zeta_{s}^{\alpha}\tilde{\zeta}_{s}^{\alpha_{0}}\delta_{s}^{-\frac{7}{3}},

(4.132)

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{M}\|_{\alpha}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\|(\mathcal{I}_{G}+\mathcal{K}_{G})^{-1}\|_{\alpha}\|\mathcal{L}\|_{0}+\|(\mathcal{I}_{G}+\mathcal{K}_{G})^{-1}\|_{0}\|\mathcal{L}\|_{\alpha}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\zeta_{s}^{\alpha}\tilde{\zeta}_{s}^{2\alpha_{0}}\delta_{s}^{-\frac{22}{5}}.

(4.133)

∎

We further have

Lemma 4.11
.

Let

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}^{\prime}=\mathcal{T}_{X}\mathcal{R}_{B}-\mathcal{T}_{X}\mathcal{R}_{G}\mathcal{N}\in\mathbf{M}_{B}^{X},\ \mathcal{Z}=\mathcal{I}_{X}-\mathcal{T}_{X}\mathcal{R}_{G}\mathcal{M}\in\mathbf{M}_{X}^{X}.

(4.134)

Then

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}\mathcal{T}_{X}=\mathcal{T}^{\prime},

(4.135)

and for
\alpha\in[0,\alpha_{1}],

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{T}^{\prime}\|_{\alpha}\lesssim\zeta_{s}^{\alpha}\tilde{\zeta}_{s}^{\alpha_{0}}\delta_{t}^{-\frac{7}{3}},\ \|\mathcal{Z}\|_{\alpha}\lesssim\zeta_{s}^{\alpha}\tilde{\zeta}_{s}^{2\alpha_{0}}\delta_{s}^{-\frac{22}{5}}.

(4.136)

Moreover,
\mathcal{R}_{B}\mathcal{T}_{X}^{-1}
is a left inverse of
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
.

Proof.

We first prove (
4.136
). We have for
\alpha\in[0,\alpha_{1}]
,

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{T}_{X}\|_{\alpha}\leq\|\varepsilon\mathcal{W}_{X}\|_{\alpha}+\|\mathcal{D}_{X}\|\lesssim 1.

From (
3.1
) and (
4.130
)–(
4.133
), we get for
\alpha\in[0,\alpha_{1}],

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{T}^{\prime}\|_{\alpha}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\mathcal{T}_{X}\|_{\alpha}+\|\mathcal{T}_{X}\|_{\alpha}\|\mathcal{N}\|_{0}+\|\mathcal{T}_{X}\|_{0}\|\mathcal{N}\|_{\alpha}\lesssim\zeta_{s}^{\alpha}\tilde{\zeta}_{s}^{\alpha_{0}}\delta_{s}^{-\frac{7}{3}},

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{Z}\|_{\alpha}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\mathcal{T}_{X}\|_{\alpha}\|\mathcal{M}\|_{0}+\|\mathcal{T}_{X}\|_{0}\|\mathcal{M}\|_{\alpha}\lesssim\zeta_{s}^{\alpha}\tilde{\zeta}_{s}^{2\alpha_{0}}\delta_{s}^{-\frac{22}{5}}.

The deduce of both (
4.135
) and the left inverse argument is similar to that of Lemma
4.6
.
∎

Lemma 4.12
.

The operator
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
defined in (
4.134
) has a left inverse
\mathcal{V}
satisfying for
\alpha\in[0,\alpha_{1}],

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{V}\|_{\alpha}\lesssim\zeta_{s+1}^{\alpha}\delta_{s+1}^{-\frac{31}{15}}.

(4.137)

Proof.

We introduce
Y=\Lambda_{\zeta_{s+1}}(\bm{k})
and let
\mathcal{E}=\mathcal{R}_{Y}\mathcal{T}^{\prime}\in\mathbf{M}_{B}^{Y}
.
We claim that
\mathcal{E}
has a left inverse
\mathcal{V}
satisfying

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{V}\|_{0}<2\delta_{s+1}^{-\frac{31}{15}}.

Let
\|\bm{l}-\bm{l}^{\prime}\|\leq\frac{{\rm diam}(Y)}{2}
and
\mathcal{P}=\mathcal{T}^{\prime}-\mathcal{E}
, which implies

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}(\bm{l},\bm{l}^{\prime})=0.

Then by recalling (
3.3
) and (
4.136
), we obtain for
\alpha\in[0,\alpha_{1}],

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{P}\|_{\alpha}

\displaystyle\lesssim({\rm diam}(Y))^{-\alpha_{1}+\alpha}\|\mathcal{P}\|_{\alpha_{1}}\leq({\rm diam}(Y))^{-\alpha_{1}+\alpha}\|\mathcal{T}^{\prime}\|_{\alpha_{1}}\lesssim\zeta_{s+1}^{-\alpha_{1}+\alpha}\zeta_{s}^{\alpha_{1}}\tilde{\zeta}_{s}^{\alpha_{0}}\delta_{s}^{-\frac{7}{3}}.

Thus by (
4.3
), (
4.70
) and
\alpha_{1}>2200\tau
, we have

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{P}\|_{0}\|\mathcal{R}_{B}\mathcal{T}_{X}^{-1}\|_{0}

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathcal{P}\|_{0}\|\mathcal{T}_{X}^{-1}\|_{0}\lesssim\zeta_{s+1}^{-\alpha_{1}}\zeta_{s}^{\alpha_{1}}\tilde{\zeta}_{s}^{\alpha_{0}}\delta_{s}^{-\frac{7}{3}}\delta_{s+1}^{-\frac{31}{15}}<\frac{1}{2}.

It then follows from Lemma
3.5
that
\mathcal{E}
has a left inverse
\mathcal{V}\in\mathbf{M}_{Y}^{B}
satisfying

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{V}\|_{0}\leq 2\|\mathcal{R}_{B}\mathcal{T}_{X}^{-1}\|_{0}<2\delta_{s+1}^{-\frac{31}{15}}.

(4.138)

If
\|\bm{l}-\bm{l}^{\prime}\|\geq 2{\rm diam}(Y)
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}(\bm{l},\bm{l}^{\prime})=0.

Using (
3.4
) and (
4.138
) yields for
\alpha\in[0,\alpha_{1}]
,

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{V}\|_{\alpha}\lesssim({\rm diam}(Y))^{\alpha}\|\mathcal{V}\|_{0}\lesssim\zeta_{s+1}^{\alpha}\delta_{s+1}^{-\frac{31}{15}}.

Finally, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}\mathcal{P}=\mathcal{R}_{B}\mathcal{V}\mathcal{R}_{Y}(\mathcal{T}^{\prime}-R_{Y}\mathcal{T}^{\prime})=0.

Since
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}=\mathcal{D}+\mathcal{R}
and
\mathcal{V}
is a left inverse of
\mathcal{E}
,
\mathcal{V}
is a left inverse of
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
.
∎

We are ready to prove Theorem
4.9
.

Proof of Theorem
4.9
.

Since (
4.123
), (
4.135
) and Lemma
4.12
, we obtain

\displaystyle\left(\mathcal{M}-\mathcal{N}\mathcal{V}\mathcal{Z}+\mathcal{V}\mathcal{Z}\right)\mathcal{T}_{X}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{R}_{G}+\mathcal{N}+(\mathcal{R}_{B}-\mathcal{N})\mathcal{V}\mathcal{T}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{R}_{G}\oplus\mathcal{R}_{B}=\mathcal{I}_{X},

which implies

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{X}^{-1}=\mathcal{M}-\mathcal{N}\mathcal{V}\mathcal{Z}+\mathcal{V}\mathcal{Z}.

Hence

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{B}\mathcal{T}_{X}^{-1}=\mathcal{V}\mathcal{Z},\ \mathcal{R}_{G}\mathcal{T}_{X}^{-1}=\mathcal{M}-\mathcal{N}\mathcal{R}_{B}\mathcal{T}_{X}^{-1}.

Then for
\alpha\in(0,\alpha_{1}]
, we can obtain by using (
3.1
), (
4.121
), (
4.136
) and (
4.137
) that

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{R}_{B}\mathcal{T}_{X}^{-1}\|_{\alpha}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\|\mathcal{V}\|_{\alpha}\|\mathcal{Z}\|_{0}+\|\mathcal{V}\|_{0}\|\mathcal{Z}\|_{\alpha}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\zeta_{s+1}^{\alpha}\tilde{\zeta}_{s}^{2\alpha_{0}}\delta_{s}^{-\frac{22}{5}}\delta_{s+1}^{-\frac{31}{15}},

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{R}_{G}\mathcal{T}_{X}^{-1}\|_{\alpha}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\mathcal{M}\|_{\alpha}+\|\mathcal{N}\|_{\alpha}\|\mathcal{R}_{B}\mathcal{T}_{X}^{-1}\|_{0}+\|\mathcal{N}\|_{0}\|\mathcal{R}_{B}\mathcal{T}_{X}^{-1}\|_{\alpha}

\displaystyle\lesssim\zeta_{s+1}^{\alpha}\tilde{\zeta}_{s}^{3\alpha_{0}}\delta_{s}^{-\frac{101}{15}}\delta_{s+1}^{-\frac{31}{15}}.

Thus for
\alpha\in(0,\alpha_{1}]
, we obtain

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{T}_{X}^{-1}\|_{\alpha}

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathcal{R}_{B}\mathcal{T}_{X}^{-1}\|_{\alpha}+\|\mathcal{R}_{G}\mathcal{T}_{X}^{-1}\|_{\alpha}

\displaystyle\lesssim\zeta_{s+1}^{\alpha}\tilde{\zeta}_{s}^{3\alpha_{0}}\delta_{s}^{-\frac{101}{15}}\delta_{s+1}^{-\frac{31}{15}}

\displaystyle<\zeta_{s+1}^{\alpha}\delta_{s+1}^{-\frac{7}{3}},

which concludes the proof of Theorem
4.9
.
∎

Step 3

:
Estimates of
\|\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda}^{-1}\|_{\alpha}
for general
(s+1)
-good
\Lambda
.

In this step, we will complete the verification of Theorem
4.2
.
Assume that the finite set
\Lambda\subset\mathbb{Z}^{d}
is
(s+1)
-
{\rm good}
, namely,

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\bm{k}^{\prime}\in Q_{s^{\prime}},\ \tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}^{\prime}}^{s^{\prime}}\subset\Lambda,\ \tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}^{\prime}}^{s^{\prime}}\subset\Omega_{\bm{k}}^{s^{\prime}+1}\Rightarrow\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{s^{\prime}+1}\subset\Lambda\ \text{for}\ s^{\prime}<s+1,\\
\{\bm{k}\in P_{s+1}:\ \tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{s+1}\subset\Lambda\}\cap Q_{s+1}=\emptyset.\end{array}\right.

It remains to verify (
4.19
) and (
4.20
) with
s
being replaced with
s+1
.

We will combine the estimates of
\mathcal{T}_{\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{s+1}}^{-1}
, smoothing property and rows estimate to finish this verification.

Theorem 4.13
.

If
\Lambda
is
(s+1)
-
{\rm good}
, then

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda}^{-1}\|_{0}

\displaystyle<\delta_{s+1}^{-\frac{2}{15}}\times\sup\limits_{\{\bm{k}\in P_{s+1}:\ \tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{s+1}\subset\Lambda\}}\left(\|\theta+\bm{k}\cdot\bm{\omega}-\theta_{s+1}\|_{\mathbb{T}}^{-1}\cdot\|\theta+\bm{k}\cdot\bm{\omega}+\theta_{s+1}\|_{\mathbb{T}}^{-1}\right),

and for
\alpha\in(0,\alpha_{1}],

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda}^{-1}\|_{\alpha}

\displaystyle<\zeta_{s+1}^{\alpha}\delta_{s+1}^{-\frac{14}{3}}.

Proof.

Define

\displaystyle\tilde{P}_{t}=\{\bm{k}\in P_{t}:\ \exists\bm{k}^{\prime}\in Q_{t-1}\ \text{s.t.}\ \tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}^{\prime}}^{t-1}\subset\Lambda,\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}^{\prime}}^{t-1}\subset\Omega_{\bm{k}}^{t}\},\ 1\leq t\leq s+1.

(4.141)

Similar to the proof of Lemma
4.9
, we have

\displaystyle\tilde{P}_{s+1}\cap Q_{s+1}=\emptyset,

and for any
\bm{l}\in\Lambda
, if

\displaystyle\bm{l}\in\bigcup_{\bm{k}\in\tilde{P}_{1}}\Omega_{\bm{k}}^{1},

then there exists a
t\in[1,s+1]
such that

\displaystyle\bm{l}\in\bigcup_{\bm{k}\in\tilde{P}_{t}\setminus Q_{t}}\Omega_{\bm{k}}^{t}.

For every
\bm{l}\in\Lambda
, define

\displaystyle U_{\bm{l}}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\Lambda_{\frac{1}{2}N_{1}}(\bm{l})\cap\Lambda,&\text{if}\ \bm{l}\notin\bigcup_{\bm{k}\in\tilde{P}_{1}}\Omega_{\bm{k}}^{1},\\
\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{t},&\text{if}\ \bm{l}\in\Omega_{\bm{k}}^{t}\ \text{for some}\ \bm{k}\in\tilde{P}_{t}\setminus Q_{t}.\end{array}\right.

Define
\mathcal{Q}_{\bm{l}}=\varepsilon\mathcal{T}_{U_{\bm{l}}}^{-1}\mathcal{W}_{\Lambda\setminus U_{\bm{l}}}^{U_{\bm{l}}}\in\mathbf{M}_{\Lambda\setminus U_{\bm{l}}}^{U_{\bm{l}}}
.
We now vary
\bm{l}\in\Lambda
and define

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}(\bm{l},\bm{l}^{\prime})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}0,&\text{for}\ \bm{l}^{\prime}\in U_{\bm{l}},\\
\mathcal{Q}_{\bm{l}}(\bm{l},\bm{l}^{\prime}),&\text{for}\ \bm{l}^{\prime}\in\Lambda\setminus U_{\bm{l}},\end{array}\right.

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}(\bm{l},\bm{l}^{\prime})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathcal{T}_{U_{\bm{l}}}^{-1}(\bm{l},\bm{l}^{\prime}),&\text{for}\ \bm{l}^{\prime}\in U_{\bm{l}}\\
0,&\text{for}\ \bm{l}^{\prime}\in\Lambda\setminus U_{\bm{l}}.\end{array}\right.

Similar to those of Theorem
4.8
, we have for
\alpha\in(0,\alpha_{1}],

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{K}\|_{0}

\displaystyle\lesssim\max\{\zeta_{1}^{\alpha_{1}}\tilde{\zeta}_{1}^{-\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{0}}\delta_{1}^{-\frac{7}{3}},N_{1}^{-\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{0}}\}\leq\frac{1}{2},

(4.142)

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{K}\|_{\alpha}

\displaystyle\lesssim\zeta_{s+1}^{\alpha}\tilde{\zeta}_{s+1}^{\alpha_{0}}\delta_{s+1}^{-\frac{7}{3}},

(4.143)

and

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{L}\|_{0}

\displaystyle\lesssim\tilde{\zeta}_{s+1}^{\alpha_{0}}\delta_{s+1}^{-\frac{1}{15}}

\displaystyle\ \ \times\sup\limits_{\{\bm{k}\in P_{s+1}:\ \tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{s+1}\subset\Lambda\}}\left(\|\theta+\bm{k}\cdot\bm{\omega}-\theta_{s+1}\|_{\mathbb{T}}^{-1}\cdot\|\theta+\bm{k}\cdot\bm{\omega}+\theta_{s+1}\|_{\mathbb{T}}^{-1}\right),

(4.144)

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{L}\|_{\alpha}

\displaystyle\lesssim\zeta_{s+1}^{\alpha}\tilde{\zeta}_{s+1}^{\alpha_{0}}\delta_{s+1}^{-\frac{7}{3}}.

(4.145)

By recalling Lemma
3.5
, we have that
\mathcal{I}_{\Lambda}+\mathcal{K}
is invertible and

\displaystyle\|(\mathcal{I}_{\Lambda}+\mathcal{K})^{-1}\|_{\alpha}

\displaystyle\lesssim\min\{1,\|\mathcal{K}\|_{\alpha}\}\ \text{for}\ \alpha\in[0,\alpha_{1}].

(4.146)

From (
4.55
), we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda}^{-1}=(\mathcal{I}_{\Lambda}+\mathcal{K})^{-1}\mathcal{L}.

By recalling (
3.1
) and (
4.142
)–(
4.146
), we have

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda}^{-1}\|_{0}

\displaystyle\leq\|(\mathcal{I}_{\Lambda}+\mathcal{K})^{-1}\|_{0}\|\mathcal{L}\|_{0}

\displaystyle<\delta_{s+1}^{-\frac{2}{15}}\times\sup\limits_{\{\bm{k}\in P_{s+1}:\ \tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{s+1}\subset\Lambda\}}\left(\|\theta+\bm{k}\cdot\bm{\omega}-\theta_{s+1}\|_{\mathbb{T}}^{-1}\cdot\|\theta+\bm{k}\cdot\bm{\omega}+\theta_{s+1}\|_{\mathbb{T}}^{-1}\right),

and for
\alpha\in(0,\alpha_{1}]
,

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda}^{-1}\|_{\alpha}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\|(\mathcal{I}_{\Lambda}+\mathcal{K})^{-1}\|_{\alpha}\|\mathcal{L}\|_{0}+\|(\mathcal{I}_{\Lambda}+\mathcal{K})^{-1}\|_{0}\|\mathcal{L}\|_{\alpha}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\zeta_{s+1}^{\alpha}\tilde{\zeta}_{s+1}^{2\alpha_{0}}\delta_{s+1}^{-\frac{22}{5}}<\zeta_{s+1}^{\alpha}\delta_{s+1}^{-\frac{14}{3}},

which completes the proof.
∎

5.
Power-law localization

In this section, we will prove Theorem
1.2
by combining Green’s function estimates and the Shnol’s theorem.

Proof of Theorem
1.2
.

Let
\varepsilon_{0}
be given by Theorem
1.1
. Fix
\theta\in\mathbb{T}\setminus\Theta_{\tau_{1}}
. Let
E\in\sigma(\mathcal{H}(\theta))
be a generalized eigenvalue of
\mathcal{H}(\theta)
and
\psi=\{\psi(\bm{n})\}_{\bm{n}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\neq 0
be the corresponding generalized eigenfunction satisfying

\displaystyle|\psi(\bm{n})|\leq(1+\|\bm{n}\|)^{d}.

From Shnol’s theorem (cf.
[
Han19
]
), it suffices to show that
\psi
decays polynomially and belongs to
\ell^{2}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})
. For this purpose, note first that there exists some
\tilde{s}\in\mathbb{N}
such that

\displaystyle\|2\theta+\bm{n}\cdot\bm{\omega}\|_{\mathbb{T}}>\frac{1}{\|n\|^{\tau_{1}}}\ \text{for all $n$ satisfying $\|n\|\geq N_{\tilde{s}}$}.

(5.1)

We claim that there exists some
s_{0}>0
such that for
s\geq s_{0}
,

\displaystyle\Lambda_{2N_{s}^{29}}\cap\left(\bigcup_{\bm{k}\in Q_{s}}\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{s}\right)\neq\emptyset.

(5.2)

Otherwise, there exists a sequence
s_{i}\rightarrow+\infty
(as
i\rightarrow\infty
) such that

\displaystyle\Lambda_{2N_{s_{i}}^{29}}\cap\left(\bigcup_{\bm{k}\in Q_{s_{i}}}\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{s_{i}}\right)=\emptyset.

(5.3)

Then we can enlarge
\tilde{\Lambda}_{N_{s_{i}}^{29}}
to
\tilde{\Lambda}_{i}
so that

\displaystyle\Lambda_{N_{s_{i}}^{29}}\subset\tilde{\Lambda}_{i}\subset\Lambda_{N_{s_{i}}^{29}+50N_{s_{i}}^{5}}

and

\displaystyle\tilde{\Lambda}_{i}\cap\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{s^{\prime}}\neq\emptyset\Rightarrow\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{s^{\prime}}\subset\tilde{\Lambda}_{i}\ \text{for $s^{\prime}\leq s_{i}$ and $\bm{k}\in P_{s^{\prime}}$}.

From (
5.3
), we have

\displaystyle\tilde{\Lambda}_{i}\cap\left(\bigcup_{\bm{k}\in Q_{s_{i}}}\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{s_{i}}\right)=\emptyset,

which shows that
\tilde{\Lambda}_{i}
is
s_{i}
-good. Let
\tilde{\Lambda}_{i,0}=\Lambda_{\frac{1}{2}N_{s_{i}}^{29}}\cap\tilde{\Lambda}_{i}
. Using Poisson’s identity yields for
\bm{n}\in\Lambda_{N_{s_{i}}}
,

\displaystyle|\psi(\bm{n})|\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{\bm{n}^{\prime}\in\tilde{\Lambda}_{i},\bm{n}^{\prime\prime}\notin\tilde{\Lambda}_{i}}|\mathcal{T}_{\tilde{\Lambda}_{i}}^{-1}(\bm{n},\bm{n}^{\prime})|\cdot|\mathcal{W}(\bm{n}^{\prime},\bm{n}^{\prime\prime})|\cdot|\psi(\bm{n}^{\prime\prime})|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(I)+(II),

where

\displaystyle(I)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\bm{n}^{\prime}\in\tilde{\Lambda}_{i,0},\bm{n}^{\prime\prime}\notin\tilde{\Lambda}_{i}}|\mathcal{T}_{\tilde{\Lambda}_{i}}^{-1}(\bm{n},\bm{n}^{\prime})|\cdot|\mathcal{W}(\bm{n}^{\prime},\bm{n}^{\prime\prime})|\cdot|\psi(\bm{n}^{\prime\prime})|,

\displaystyle(II)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\bm{n}^{\prime}\in\tilde{\Lambda}_{i}\setminus\tilde{\Lambda}_{i,0},\bm{n}^{\prime\prime}\notin\tilde{\Lambda}_{i}}|\mathcal{T}_{\tilde{\Lambda}_{i}}^{-1}(\bm{n},\bm{n}^{\prime})|\cdot|\mathcal{W}(\bm{n}^{\prime},\bm{n}^{\prime\prime})|\cdot|\psi(\bm{n}^{\prime\prime})|.

For
(I)
, we have by Theorem
1.1
, (
3.8
), and
|\psi(\bm{n})|\leq(1+\|\bm{n}\|)^{d}
that

\displaystyle(I)

\displaystyle\lesssim(N_{s_{i}}^{29})^{-\alpha_{1}+2d}\|\mathcal{T}_{\tilde{\Lambda}_{i}}\|_{d}\|\mathcal{W}\|_{\alpha_{1}}

\displaystyle<\delta_{s_{i}}^{\frac{\alpha_{1}}{36}}\rightarrow 0\ \text{as $i\rightarrow\infty$}.

For
(II)
, we also have by Theorem
1.1
, (
3.8
) and
|\psi(\bm{n})|\leq(1+\|\bm{n}\|)^{d}
that

\displaystyle(II)

\displaystyle\lesssim(N_{s_{i}}^{29})^{-\alpha_{1}+2d}\|\mathcal{T}_{\tilde{\Lambda}_{i}}^{-1}\|_{\alpha_{1}}\|\mathcal{W}\|_{d}

\displaystyle<\delta_{s_{i}}^{\frac{\alpha_{1}}{36}}\rightarrow 0\ \text{as $i\rightarrow\infty$}.

It follows that
\psi(\bm{n})=0\ {\rm for}\ \forall\bm{n}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
, which contradicts
\psi\neq 0
. And the Claim is proved.

Next define

\displaystyle U_{s}=\Lambda_{8N_{s+1}^{29}}\setminus\Lambda_{4N_{s}^{29}},\ U_{s}^{*}=\Lambda_{10N_{s+1}^{29}}\setminus\Lambda_{3N_{s}^{29}}.

We can also enlarge
U_{s}^{*}
to
\tilde{U}_{s}^{*}
so that

\displaystyle U_{s}^{*}\subset\tilde{U}_{s}^{*}\subset\Lambda_{50N_{s}^{5}}(U_{s}^{*})

and

\displaystyle\tilde{U}_{s}^{*}\cap\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{s^{\prime}}\neq\emptyset\Rightarrow\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{s^{\prime}}\subset\tilde{U}_{s}^{*}\ \text{for $s^{\prime}\leq s$ and $\bm{k}\in P_{s^{\prime}}$}.

Let
\bm{n}
satisfy
\|\bm{n}\|>\max(4N_{\tilde{s}}^{29},4N_{s_{0}}^{29})
. Then there exists some
s\geq\max(\tilde{s},s_{0})
such that

\displaystyle\bm{n}\in U_{s}\subset\tilde{U}_{s}^{*}.

(5.4)

Without loss of generality and since (
5.2
), we can assume

\displaystyle\Lambda_{2N_{s}^{29}}\cap\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{s}\neq\emptyset

for some
\bm{k}\in Q_{s}^{+}
. Then for
\bm{k}\neq\bm{k}^{\prime}\in Q_{s}^{+}
, we have

\displaystyle\|\bm{k}-\bm{k}^{\prime}\|>\left(\frac{\gamma}{2\delta_{s}}\right)^{\frac{1}{\tau}}\gtrsim N_{s+1}^{30}\gg{\rm diam}(\tilde{U}_{s}^{*}).

Therefore,

\displaystyle\tilde{U}_{s}^{*}\cap\left(\bigcup_{\bm{l}\in Q_{s}^{+}}\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{l}}^{s}\right)=\emptyset.

Now, if there exists some
\bm{l}\in Q_{s}^{-}
such that

\displaystyle\tilde{U}_{s}^{*}\cap\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{l}}^{s}\neq\emptyset,

then

\displaystyle N_{s}\leq N_{s}^{29}-100N_{s}^{5}\leq\|\bm{l}\|-\|\bm{k}\|\leq\|\bm{l}+\bm{k}\|\leq\|\bm{l}\|+\|\bm{k}\|<11N_{s+1}^{29}.

Recalling

\displaystyle Q_{s}\subset P_{s}\subset\mathbb{Z}^{d}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=0}^{s-1}\bm{l}_{i},

we have
\bm{l}+\bm{k}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
. According to (
5.1
), we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(11N_{s+1}^{29})^{\tau_{1}}}<\|2\theta+(\bm{l}+\bm{k})\cdot\bm{\omega}\|_{\mathbb{T}}\leq\|\theta+\bm{l}\cdot\bm{\omega}-\theta_{s}\|_{\mathbb{T}}+\|\theta+\bm{k}\cdot\bm{\omega}+\theta_{s}\|_{\mathbb{T}}<2\delta_{s},

which contradicts

\displaystyle\delta_{s}^{-1}\gtrsim N_{s+1}^{30\tau}\gg N_{s+1}^{29\tau_{1}}.

We thus have shown

\displaystyle\tilde{U}_{s}^{*}\cap\left(\bigcup_{\bm{l}\in Q_{s}}\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{l}}^{s}\right)=\emptyset.

This implies that
\tilde{U}_{s}^{*}
is
s
-
{\rm good}
.

Finally, by recalling (
5.4
), we can set

\displaystyle\hat{U}_{s}=\Lambda_{\frac{1}{2}N_{s}^{29}}(U_{s}).

Then

\displaystyle|\psi(\bm{n})|\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{\bm{n}^{\prime}\in\tilde{U}_{s}^{*},\bm{n}^{\prime\prime}\notin\tilde{U}_{s}^{*}}|\mathcal{T}_{\tilde{U}_{s}^{*}}^{-1}(\bm{n},\bm{n}^{\prime})|\cdot|\mathcal{W}(\bm{n}^{\prime},\bm{n}^{\prime\prime})|\cdot|\psi(\bm{n}^{\prime\prime})|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(III)+(IV),

where

\displaystyle(III)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\bm{n}^{\prime}\in\hat{U}_{s},\bm{n}^{\prime\prime}\notin\tilde{U}_{s}^{*}}|\mathcal{T}_{\tilde{U}_{s}^{*}}^{-1}(\bm{n},\bm{n}^{\prime})|\cdot|\mathcal{W}(\bm{n}^{\prime},\bm{n}^{\prime\prime})|\cdot|\psi(\bm{n}^{\prime\prime})|

\displaystyle(IV)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\bm{n}^{\prime}\in\tilde{U}_{s}^{*}\setminus\hat{U}_{s},\bm{n}^{\prime\prime}\notin\tilde{U}_{s}^{*}}|\mathcal{T}_{\tilde{U}_{s}^{*}}^{-1}(\bm{n},\bm{n}^{\prime})|\cdot|\mathcal{W}(\bm{n}^{\prime},\bm{n}^{\prime\prime})|\cdot|\psi(\bm{n}^{\prime\prime})|.

For
(III)
, we have by Theorem
1.1
, (
3.8
) and
|\psi(\bm{n})|\leq(1+\|\bm{n}\|)^{d}
that

\displaystyle(III)

\displaystyle\lesssim(N_{s}^{29})^{-\alpha_{1}+d}\|\mathcal{T}_{\tilde{\Lambda}_{i}}\|_{d}\|\mathcal{W}\|_{\alpha_{1}}(1+\|\bm{n}\|)^{d}

\displaystyle\lesssim(N_{s+1})^{\frac{29}{30}(-\alpha_{1}+2d)+140\tau}(1+\|\bm{n}\|)^{d}.

For
(IV)
, we also have by Theorem
1.1
, (
3.8
) and
|\psi(\bm{n})|\leq(1+\|\bm{n}\|)^{d}
that

\displaystyle(IV)

\displaystyle\lesssim(N_{s}^{29})^{-\alpha_{1}+d}\|\mathcal{T}_{\tilde{\Lambda}_{i}}^{-1}\|_{\alpha_{1}}\|\mathcal{W}\|_{d}(1+\|\bm{n}\|)^{d}

\displaystyle\lesssim(N_{s+1})^{-\frac{13}{15}\alpha_{1}+\frac{19}{15}d+140\tau}(1+\|\bm{n}\|)^{d}.

Combining the above estimates and since
\alpha_{1}>2200\tau
,
\|\bm{n}\|\leq 8N_{s+1}^{29}
, we have

\displaystyle|\psi(\bm{n})|<(1+\|\bm{n}\|)^{-\frac{\alpha_{1}}{60}}.

We complete the proof of arithmetic power-law localization.
∎

6.
Dynamical localization

In this section, we prove Theorem
1.3
concerning dynamical localization.

Proof of Theorem
1.3
.

Since power-law localization holds true for
\theta\in\Theta_{\tau_{1},A}^{*}
by Theorem
1.2
, let
\{\varphi_{\bm{q}}(\theta),E_{\bm{q}}(\theta)\}_{\bm{q}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}
denote a complete set of eigenfunctions and corresponding eigenvalues of
\mathcal{H}(\theta)
. For simplicity, we omit all dependences on
\theta
. Then

\displaystyle\delta_{\bm{0}}=\sum_{\bm{q}}\varphi_{\bm{q}}(\bm{0})\varphi_{\bm{q}}

and hence

\displaystyle e^{\sqrt{-1}t\mathcal{H}}\delta_{\bm{0}}=\sum_{\bm{q}}e^{\sqrt{-1}tE_{\bm{q}}}\varphi_{\bm{q}}(\bm{0})\varphi_{\bm{q}}.

Thus, it suffices to estimate

\displaystyle\sum_{\bm{q}}\left(\sum_{\bm{x}}(1+\|\bm{x}\|)^{p}|\varphi_{\bm{q}}(\bm{x})|\right)|\varphi_{\bm{q}}(\bm{0})|.

(6.1)

Let
I_{0}=\emptyset
and
I_{j}=\{\bm{q}:\ |\varphi_{\bm{q}}(\bm{0})|>N_{j}^{-15\alpha_{1}}\}\ (j\geq 1)
. Then

\displaystyle\eqref{dyn}=\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\sum_{\bm{q}\in I_{j}\setminus I_{j-1}}\left(\sum_{\bm{x}}(1+\|\bm{x}\|)^{p}|\varphi_{\bm{q}}(\bm{x})|\right)|\varphi_{\bm{q}}(\bm{0})|.

(6.2)

We claim that for
\bm{q}\in I_{j}
and
s\geq j
,

\displaystyle\Lambda_{2N_{s}^{29}}\cap\left(\bigcup_{\bm{k}\in Q_{s}}\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{s}\right)\neq\emptyset.

(6.3)

Otherwise, there exists some
s
-
{\rm good}
set
\Lambda
such that

\displaystyle\Lambda_{N_{s}^{29}}\subset\Lambda\subset\Lambda_{N_{s}^{29}+50N_{s}^{5}}.

Then since Theorem
1.1
and (
3.8
), we get a contradiction with

\displaystyle|\varphi_{\bm{q}}(\bm{0})|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\bm{n}^{\prime}\in\Lambda,\bm{n}^{\prime\prime}\notin\Lambda}|\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda}^{-1}(\bm{0},\bm{n}^{\prime})|\cdot|\mathcal{W}(\bm{n}^{\prime},\bm{n}^{\prime\prime})|\cdot|\varphi_{\bm{q}}(\bm{n}^{\prime\prime})|

\displaystyle<N_{s}^{-15\alpha_{1}}\leq N_{j}^{-15\alpha_{1}}.

Now assume

\displaystyle\delta_{m}^{\frac{1}{30}}<A\leq\delta_{m-1}^{\frac{1}{30}}\ (\delta_{-1}:=+\infty).

(6.4)

If
\bm{q}\in I_{j}
, then by (
1.8
) and similar to the proof of Theorem
1.2
, we can prove that for
s\geq\max(m,j)
, there is no bad (enlarged resonant) block of the
s
-th induction step inside
\Lambda_{10N_{s+1}^{29}}\setminus\Lambda_{3N_{s}^{29}}
, which proves
|\varphi_{\bm{q}}(\bm{x})|<(1+\|\bm{x}\|)^{-\frac{\alpha_{1}}{60}}
for
\|\bm{x}\|\geq\max(4N_{m}^{29},4N_{j}^{29})
. From the Hilbert-Schmidt argument, we have

\displaystyle C(d)N_{\max(m,j)}^{29d}

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{\|x\|\leq 4N_{\max(m,j)}^{29}}\sum_{\bm{q}}|\varphi_{\bm{q}}(\bm{x})|^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{\bm{q}\in I_{j}}\sum_{\|\bm{x}\|\leq 4N_{\max(m,j)}^{29}}|\varphi_{\bm{q}}(\bm{x})|^{2}

\displaystyle=\#I_{j}-\sum_{\bm{q}\in I_{j}}\sum_{\|\bm{x}\|>4N_{\max(m,j)}^{29}}|\varphi_{\bm{q}}(\bm{x})|^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\#I_{j}.

Thus
\#I_{j}\leq C(d)N_{\max(m,j)}^{29d}
.

To estimate (
6.2
), using
|\varphi_{\bm{q}}(\bm{x})|<(1+\|\bm{x}\|)^{-\frac{\alpha_{1}}{60}}
for
\bm{q}\in I_{m}
and
\|\bm{x}\|\geq 4N_{m}^{29}
implies

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{m}\sum_{\bm{q}\in I_{j}\setminus I_{j-1}}\left(\sum_{\bm{x}}(1+\|\bm{x}\|)^{p}|\varphi_{\bm{q}}(\bm{x})|\right)|\varphi_{\bm{q}}(\bm{0})|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{\bm{q}\in I_{m}}\left(\sum_{\bm{x}}(1+\|\bm{x}\|)^{p}|\varphi_{\bm{q}}(\bm{x})|\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\#I_{m}\cdot\sup_{\bm{q}\in I_{m}}\left(\sum_{\|\bm{x}\|\leq 4N_{m}^{29}}+\sum_{\|\bm{x}\|>4N_{m}^{29}}\right)(1+\|\bm{x}\|)^{p}|\varphi_{\bm{q}}(\bm{x})|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(\alpha_{1},p,d)N_{m}^{29(p+2d)}.

(6.5)

Using
|\varphi_{\bm{q}}(\bm{x})|<N_{j}^{-15\alpha_{1}}\lesssim(1+\|\bm{x}\|)^{-\frac{\alpha_{1}}{60}}
for
j\geq m
,
\bm{q}\in I_{j}
and
\|\bm{x}\|\geq 4N_{j}^{29}
yields

\displaystyle\sum_{\bm{q}\in I_{j}\setminus I_{j-1}}\left(\sum_{\bm{x}}(1+\|\bm{x}\|)^{p}|\varphi_{\bm{q}}(\bm{x})|\right)|\varphi_{\bm{q}}(\bm{0})|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\#I_{j}\cdot\sup_{q\in I_{j}}\left(\sum_{\|\bm{x}\|\leq 4N_{j}^{29}}+\sum_{\|\bm{x}\|>4N_{j}^{29}}\right)(1+\|\bm{x}\|)^{p}\cdot|\varphi_{\bm{q}}(\bm{x})|\cdot N_{j-1}^{-15\alpha_{1}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(\alpha_{1},p,d)(N_{j}^{29})^{p+2d}N_{j-1}^{-15\alpha_{1}}\leq C(\alpha_{1},p,d)N_{j}^{-\frac{\alpha_{1}}{2}+30p+60d},

where
N_{0}=1
. Summing up
j
for
j\geq m
gives

\displaystyle\sum_{j=m}^{\infty}\sum_{\bm{q}\in I_{j}\setminus I_{j-1}}\left(\sum_{\bm{x}}(1+\|\bm{x}\|)^{p}|\varphi_{\bm{q}}(\bm{x})|\right)|\varphi_{\bm{q}}(\bm{0})|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}C(\alpha_{1},p,d)N_{m}^{-\frac{\alpha_{1}}{2}+30p+60d}&\text{if $m\geq 1$},\\
C(\alpha_{1},p,d)N_{1}^{29(p+2d)}&\text{if $m=0$}.\end{array}\right.

From (
6.5
) and (
6
), we obtain

(
6.2
)

\displaystyle\leq C(\alpha_{1},p,d)\max(N_{m}^{29(p+2d)},N_{1}^{29(p+2d)})

\displaystyle\leq C(\alpha_{1},p,d)\max(A^{-\frac{29(p+2d)}{\tau}},\varepsilon_{0}^{-\frac{29(p+2d)}{\tau}}),

where we have used (
6.4
).

Hence we finish the proof of dynamical localization.
∎

7.
\left(\frac{1}{2}-\right)
Hölder contuinity of the IDS

In this section, we prove the finite volume version of the
\left(\frac{1}{2}-\right)
-Hölder continuity of the IDS.

Proof of Thoerem
1.4
.

Let
\mathcal{T}
be given by (
4.2
). Fix
\theta\in\mathbb{T}
,
E\in\mathbb{R}
and
\mu>0
as in Theorem
1.4
. Let
\varepsilon_{0}
be such that Theorem
1.1
holds true for
0<|\varepsilon|<\varepsilon_{0}
. Let

\displaystyle 0<\eta<\eta_{0}=\delta_{1}^{\frac{17}{135\mu}}.

(7.1)

Denote by
\{\xi_{r}:\ r=1,\cdots,R\}\subset{\rm span}_{\mathbb{R}}\{\delta_{\bm{n}}:\ \bm{n}\in\Lambda_{N}\}
the
\ell^{2}
-orthonormal eigenvectors of
\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda_{N}}
with eigenvalues belonging to
[-\eta,\eta]
. We aim to prove that for sufficiently large
N
(depending on
\eta
),

\displaystyle R\leq(\#\Lambda_{N})\eta^{\frac{1}{2}-\mu}.

From (
7.1
), we can choose
s\geq 1
such that

\displaystyle\delta_{s+1}^{\frac{17}{135\mu}}\leq\eta<\delta_{s}^{\frac{17}{135\mu}}.

(7.2)

Enlarge
\Lambda_{N}
to
\tilde{\Lambda}_{N}
so that

\displaystyle\Lambda_{N}\subset\tilde{\Lambda}_{N}\subset\Lambda_{N+50N_{s}^{5}}

and

\displaystyle\tilde{\Lambda}_{N}\cap\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{s^{\prime}}\neq\emptyset\Rightarrow\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{s^{\prime}}\subset\tilde{\Lambda}_{N}\ \text{for}\ s^{\prime}\leq s\ \text{and}\ \bm{k}\in P_{s^{\prime}}.

Define further

\displaystyle\mathscr{K}=\left\{\bm{k}\in P_{s}\cap\tilde{\Lambda}_{N}:\ \min_{\sigma=\pm 1}(\|\theta+\bm{k}\cdot\bm{\omega}+\sigma\theta_{s}\|_{\mathbb{T}})<\eta^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\mu}{2}}\right\}

and

\displaystyle\tilde{\Lambda}_{N}^{{}^{\prime}}=\tilde{\Lambda}_{N}\setminus\bigcup_{\bm{k}\in P_{s}\cap\tilde{\Lambda}_{N}}\Omega_{\bm{k}}^{s},\ \tilde{\Lambda}_{N}^{{}^{\prime\prime}}=\tilde{\Lambda}_{N}\setminus\bigcup_{\bm{k}\in\mathscr{K}}\Omega_{\bm{k}}^{s}.

Thus
\tilde{\Lambda}_{N}^{{}^{\prime}}\subset\tilde{\Lambda}_{N}^{{}^{\prime\prime}}
and

\displaystyle\tilde{\Lambda}_{N}^{{}^{\prime\prime}}\setminus\tilde{\Lambda}_{N}^{{}^{\prime}}\subset\bigcup_{\bm{k}\in(P_{s}\cap\tilde{\Lambda}_{N})\setminus\mathscr{K}}\Omega_{\bm{k}}^{s}\subset\bigcup_{\bm{k}\in(P_{s}\cap\tilde{\Lambda}_{N})\setminus\mathscr{K}}\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{s}\subset\tilde{\Lambda}_{N}^{{}^{\prime\prime}}.

Define

\displaystyle\tilde{P}_{t}=\{\bm{k}\in P_{t}\cap\tilde{\Lambda}_{N}:\ \exists\bm{k}^{\prime}\in Q_{t-1}\ \text{s.t.}\ \tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}^{\prime}}^{t-1}\subset\tilde{\Lambda}_{N}^{{}^{\prime}},\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}^{\prime}}^{t-1}\subset\Omega_{\bm{k}}^{t}\},\ t\in[1,s].

Similar to the proof of Lemma
4.9
, we have

\displaystyle\tilde{P}_{s}=\emptyset,

and for any
\bm{l}\in\tilde{\Lambda}_{N}^{{}^{\prime}}
, if

\displaystyle\bm{l}\in\bigcup_{\bm{j}\in\tilde{P}_{1}}\Omega_{\bm{k}}^{1},

there exists some
t\in[1,s-1]
such that

\displaystyle\bm{l}\in\bigcup_{\bm{k}\in\tilde{P}_{t}\setminus Q_{t}}\Omega_{\bm{k}}^{t}.

For every
\bm{l}\in\tilde{\Lambda}_{N}^{{}^{\prime\prime}}
, define

\displaystyle U_{\bm{l}}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\Lambda_{\frac{1}{2}N_{1}}(\bm{l})\cap\tilde{\Lambda}_{N}^{{}^{\prime\prime}},&\text{if}\ \bm{l}\notin\bigcup_{\bm{k}\in\left(\tilde{P}_{1}\cup(P_{s}\cap\tilde{\Lambda}_{N})\right)\setminus\mathscr{K}}\Omega_{\bm{k}}^{1},\\
\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{t},&\text{if}\ \bm{l}\in\Omega_{\bm{k}}^{t}\ \text{for some}\ \bm{k}\in\tilde{P}_{t}\setminus Q_{t},\\
\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{s},&\text{if}\ \bm{l}\in\Omega_{\bm{k}}^{s}\ \text{for some}\ \bm{k}\in(P_{s}\cap\tilde{\Lambda}_{N})\setminus\mathscr{K}.\end{array}\right.

Define
\mathcal{Q}_{\bm{l}}=\varepsilon\mathcal{T}_{U_{\bm{l}}}^{-1}\mathcal{W}_{\tilde{\Lambda}_{N}^{{}^{\prime\prime}}\setminus U_{\bm{l}}}^{U_{\bm{l}}}\in\mathbf{M}_{\tilde{\Lambda}_{N}^{{}^{\prime\prime}}\setminus U_{\bm{l}}}^{U_{\bm{l}}}
.
Varying
\bm{l}\in\tilde{\Lambda}_{N}^{{}^{\prime\prime}}
leads to

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}(\bm{l},\bm{l}^{\prime})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}0,&\text{for}\ \bm{l}^{\prime}\in U_{\bm{l}}\\
\mathcal{Q}_{\bm{l}}(\bm{l},\bm{l}^{\prime}),&\text{for}\ \bm{l}^{\prime}\in\tilde{\Lambda}_{N}^{{}^{\prime\prime}}\setminus U_{\bm{l}},\end{array}\right.

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}(\bm{l},\bm{l}^{\prime})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathcal{T}_{U_{\bm{l}}}^{-1}(\bm{l},\bm{l}^{\prime}),&\text{for}\ \bm{l}^{\prime}\in U_{\bm{l}}\\
0,&\text{for}\ \bm{l}^{\prime}\in\tilde{\Lambda}_{N}^{{}^{\prime\prime}}\setminus U_{\bm{l}}.\end{array}\right.

We estimate
\mathcal{K}\in\mathbf{M}_{\tilde{\Lambda}_{N}^{{}^{\prime\prime}}}^{\tilde{\Lambda}_{N}^{{}^{\prime\prime}}}
and
\mathcal{L}\in\mathbf{M}_{\tilde{\Lambda}_{N}^{{}^{\prime\prime}}}^{\tilde{\Lambda}_{N}^{{}^{\prime\prime}}}
. Similar to the proof of Lemma
4.9
, we can obtain

(1) If
\bm{l}\notin\bigcup_{\bm{k}\in\left(\tilde{P}_{1}\cup(P_{s}\cap\tilde{\Lambda}_{N})\right)\setminus\mathscr{K}}\Omega_{\bm{k}}^{1}
, then

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{K}^{\{\bm{l}\}}\|_{\alpha_{0}}\lesssim N_{1}^{-\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{0}},\ \|\mathcal{L}^{\{\bm{l}\}}\|_{\alpha_{0}}\lesssim\delta_{0}^{-2}.

(2) If
\bm{l}\in\Omega_{\bm{k}}^{t}
for some
\bm{k}\in\tilde{P}_{t}\setminus Q_{t}
and
t\in[1,s-1]
, then

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{K}^{\{\bm{l}\}}\|_{\alpha_{0}}\lesssim\zeta_{t}^{\alpha_{1}}\tilde{\zeta}_{t}^{-\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{0}}\delta_{t}^{-\frac{7}{3}},\ \|\mathcal{L}^{\{\bm{l}\}}\|_{\alpha_{0}}\lesssim\tilde{\zeta}_{t}^{\alpha_{0}}\delta_{t}^{-\frac{31}{15}}.

(3) If
\bm{l}\in\Omega_{\bm{k}}^{s}
for some
\bm{k}\in(P_{s}\cap\tilde{\Lambda}_{N})\setminus\mathscr{K}
, then by (
3.1
) and Remark
4.3
, we get

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{Q}_{\bm{l}}\|_{\alpha_{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\mathcal{T}_{U_{\bm{l}}}^{-1}\|_{\alpha_{1}}\|\mathcal{W}_{\Lambda}\|_{\alpha_{1}}\lesssim\zeta_{s}^{\alpha_{1}}\delta_{s}^{-\frac{1}{3}}\eta^{\mu-1}.

(7.3)

Note that if
\bm{l}^{\prime}\in\Lambda\setminus U_{\bm{l}}
, then
\|\bm{l}-\bm{l}^{\prime}\|\geq\frac{\tilde{\zeta}_{s}}{2}
. This implies
\mathcal{K}^{\{\bm{l}\}}(\bm{l},\bm{l}^{\prime})=0
for
\|\bm{l}-\bm{l}^{\prime}\|<\frac{\tilde{\zeta}_{s}}{2}
. By (
3.3
) and (
7.3
), we obtain

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{K}^{\{\bm{l}\}}\|_{\alpha_{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\tilde{\zeta}_{s}^{-\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{0}}\|\mathcal{K}^{\{\bm{l}\}}\|_{\alpha_{1}}\leq\tilde{\zeta}_{s}^{-\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{0}}\|\mathcal{Q}_{\bm{l}}\|_{\alpha_{1}}\lesssim\zeta_{s}^{\alpha_{1}}\tilde{\zeta}_{s}^{-\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{0}}\delta_{s}^{-\frac{1}{3}}\eta^{\mu-1}.

By the definition of
U_{\bm{l}}
, if
\|\bm{l}-\bm{l}^{\prime}\|>2\tilde{\zeta}_{s}
, then
l^{\prime}\notin U_{\bm{l}}
. This implies
\mathcal{L}^{\{\bm{l}\}}(\bm{l},\bm{l}^{\prime})=0
for
\|\bm{l}-\bm{l}^{\prime}\|>2\tilde{\zeta}_{s}
. By (
3.4
) and (
4.17
), we have

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{L}^{\{\bm{l}\}}\|_{\alpha_{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\tilde{\zeta}_{s}^{\alpha_{0}}\|\mathcal{L}^{\{\bm{l}\}}\|_{0}\leq\tilde{\zeta}_{s}^{\alpha_{0}}\|\mathcal{T}_{U_{\bm{l}}}^{-1}\|_{0}

\displaystyle\lesssim\tilde{\zeta}_{s}^{\alpha_{0}}\delta_{s}^{-\frac{1}{15}}\|\theta+\bm{k}\cdot\bm{\omega}-\theta_{s}\|_{\mathbb{T}}^{-1}\cdot\|\theta+\bm{k}\cdot\bm{\omega}+\theta_{s}\|_{\mathbb{T}}^{-1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\tilde{\zeta}_{s}^{\alpha_{0}}\delta_{s}^{-\frac{1}{15}}\eta^{\mu-1}.

By (
3.5
), (
7.2
) and the definition of
\mu
, we can get

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{K}\|_{0}\lesssim

\displaystyle\sup_{\bm{l}\in\tilde{\Lambda}_{N}^{{}^{\prime\prime}}}\|\mathcal{K}^{\{\bm{l}\}}\|_{\alpha_{0}}\lesssim\max\{\zeta_{s}^{\alpha_{1}}\tilde{\zeta}_{s}^{-\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{0}}\delta_{s}^{-\frac{1}{3}}\eta^{\mu-1},\zeta_{1}^{\alpha_{1}}\tilde{\zeta}_{1}^{-\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{0}}\delta_{1}^{-\frac{7}{3}},N_{1}^{-\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{0}}\}\leq\frac{1}{2},

and

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{L}\|_{0}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sup_{\bm{l}\in\tilde{\Lambda}_{N}^{{}^{\prime\prime}}}\|\mathcal{L}^{\{\bm{l}\}}\|_{\alpha_{0}}\lesssim\tilde{\zeta}_{s}^{\alpha_{0}}\delta_{s}^{-\frac{1}{15}}\eta^{\mu-1}.

Recalling Lemma
3.5
, we have that
\mathcal{I}_{\tilde{\Lambda}_{N}^{{}^{\prime\prime}}}+\mathcal{K}
is invertible and

\displaystyle\|(\mathcal{I}_{\tilde{\Lambda}_{N}^{{}^{\prime\prime}}}+\mathcal{K})^{-1}\|_{0}

\displaystyle\leq 2.

From (
4.55
), we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{\tilde{\Lambda}_{N}^{{}^{\prime\prime}}}^{-1}=(\mathcal{I}_{\tilde{\Lambda}_{N}^{{}^{\prime\prime}}}+\mathcal{K})^{-1}\mathcal{L}.

Recalling (
3.1
), we have

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{T}_{\tilde{\Lambda}_{N}^{{}^{\prime\prime}}}^{-1}\|_{0}

\displaystyle\leq\|(\mathcal{I}_{\tilde{\Lambda}_{N}^{{}^{\prime\prime}}}+\mathcal{K})^{-1}\|_{0}\|\mathcal{L}\|_{0}

\displaystyle\lesssim\tilde{\zeta}_{s}^{\alpha_{0}}\delta_{s}^{-\frac{1}{15}}\eta^{\mu-1}

\displaystyle<\frac{1}{2}\delta_{s}^{-\frac{13}{180}}\eta^{\mu-1}<\frac{1}{2}\eta^{-1},

(7.4)

where the last inequality follows from (
7.1
) and (
7.2
).

Finally, similar to the proof in
[
CSZ24b
]
, we obtain

\displaystyle R\lesssim N_{s}^{3d}\cdot\eta^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\mu}{2}}\#\Lambda_{N}\leq\eta^{\frac{1}{2}-\mu}\#\Lambda_{N}.

We finish the proof of Theorem
1.4
.
∎

8.
Absence of eigenvaalues

To accomplish the proof of Theorem
1.5
, we first need

Lemma 8.1
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
1.1
, for a.e.
\theta\in\mathbb{T}
, there is an integer
s_{0}(\theta)>0
such that
Q_{s}\cap\Lambda_{2N_{s}^{29}}=\emptyset
for
s\geq s_{0}(\theta)
.

Proof.

Define

\displaystyle B^{s}=\{\theta\in\mathbb{T}:\ Q_{s}\cap\Lambda_{2N_{s}^{29}}\neq\emptyset\}.

and
\mathscr{K}=\cap_{i=0}^{\infty}\cup_{j\geq i}B^{i}
. It suffices to show that
\mathscr{K}
has zero Lebesgue measure. For this purpose, write

\displaystyle B_{\bm{k}}^{s}=\{\theta\in\mathbb{T}:\ \bm{k}\in Q_{s}\cap\Lambda_{2N_{s}^{29}}\}\subset\{\theta\in\mathbb{T}:\ \bm{k}\in\Lambda_{2N_{s}^{29}},\min_{\sigma=\pm 1}\|\theta+\bm{k}\cdot\bm{\omega}+\sigma\theta_{s}\|_{\mathbb{T}}<\delta_{s}\}.

Then
B^{s}=\cup_{\bm{k}}B_{\bm{k}}^{s}
. By the Borel-Cantelli theorem, it remains to prove
\sum\limits_{s\geq 0}m(B^{s})<\infty
, where
m(\cdot)
is the Lebesgue measure. Indeed, since

\displaystyle m(B_{\bm{k}}^{s})\leq m(\{\theta\in\mathbb{T}:\ \bm{k}\in\Lambda_{2N_{s}^{29}},\min_{\sigma=\pm 1}\|\theta+\bm{k}\cdot\bm{\omega}+\sigma\theta_{s}\|_{\mathbb{T}}<\delta_{s}\})<4\delta_{s},

we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{s}m(B^{s})\leq\sum_{s}(\sum_{\bm{k}}m(B_{\bm{k}}^{s}))\leq C(d)\sum_{s}(N_{s}^{29d}\delta_{s})<+\infty.

This proves Lemma
8.1
.
∎

Now we can prove Theorem
1.5
.

Proof of Theorem
1.5
.

Suppose
\tilde{\mathcal{H}}
has some eigenvalue
E
. Then there must be
\psi=\{\psi_{l}\}_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}\in\ell^{2}(\mathbb{Z}),\ \psi\neq 0
so that

\displaystyle\sum_{l^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}}\hat{v}(l-l^{\prime})\psi_{l^{\prime}}+(\varepsilon u(\bm{x}+l\bm{\omega})-E)\psi_{l}=0.

Define

\displaystyle F(\theta)=\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}\psi_{l}e^{2\pi\sqrt{-1}l\theta}

and

\displaystyle\xi_{\bm{n}}(\theta)=e^{2\pi\sqrt{-1}\bm{n}\cdot\bm{x}}F(\theta+\bm{n}\cdot\bm{\omega}).

We have

\displaystyle\|F\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}=\|\psi\|_{\ell^{2}(\mathbb{Z})}>0

(8.1)

and

\displaystyle(v(\theta)-E)F(\theta)+\varepsilon\sum_{\bm{k}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\phi(\bm{k})\xi_{\bm{k}}(\theta)=0.

(8.2)

Then

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{T}}\sum_{\bm{n}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\frac{|\xi_{\bm{n}}(\theta)|^{2}}{(1+\|\bm{n}\|)^{2d}}d\theta=\sum_{\bm{n}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\frac{\|F\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}^{2}}{(1+\|\bm{n}\|)^{2d}}\leq C(d)\|F\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}^{2}<+\infty.

This implies that there is a set
\mathscr{L}
with
m(\mathscr{L})=0
such that for
\theta\in\mathbb{T}\setminus\mathscr{L}
, we have
\sum_{\bm{n}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\frac{|\xi_{\bm{n}}(\theta)|^{2}}{(1+\|\bm{n}\|)^{2d}}<+\infty
and

\displaystyle|\xi_{\bm{n}}(\theta)|\leq C(\theta,d)(1+\|\bm{n}\|)^{d},\ C(\theta,d)>0.

(8.3)

Now we let
\theta=\theta+\bm{n}\cdot\bm{\omega}
in (
8.2
). Then

\displaystyle(v(\theta+\bm{n}\cdot\bm{\omega})-E)F(\theta+\bm{n}\cdot\bm{\omega})+\varepsilon\sum_{\bm{k}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\phi(\bm{k})e^{2\pi\sqrt{-1}\bm{k}\cdot\bm{x}}F(\theta+(\bm{n}+\bm{k})\cdot\bm{\omega})=0.

Multiplying by
e^{2\pi\sqrt{-1}\bm{n}\cdot\bm{x}}
in the above equality implies

\displaystyle(v(\theta+\bm{n}\cdot\bm{\omega})-E)\xi_{\bm{n}}(\theta)+\varepsilon\sum_{\bm{k}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\phi(\bm{n}-\bm{k})\xi_{\bm{k}}(\theta)=0.

Moreover,
\mathcal{H}(\theta)\xi(\theta)=E\xi(\theta)
with
\xi(\theta)=\{\xi_{\bm{n}}(\theta)\}_{\bm{n}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}
. Hence, we have by using the Poisson’s identity for
\bm{n}\in\Lambda\subset\mathbb{Z}^{d}
,

\displaystyle\xi_{\bm{n}}(\theta)=-\varepsilon\sum_{\bm{n}^{\prime}\in\Lambda,\bm{n}^{\prime\prime}\notin\Lambda}\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda}^{-1}(E;\theta)(\bm{n},\bm{n}^{\prime})\cdot\phi(\bm{n}^{\prime}-\bm{n}^{\prime\prime})\cdot\xi_{\bm{n}^{\prime\prime}}(\theta).

(8.4)

By (
1.7
), we can enlarge
{\Lambda}_{N_{s}^{29}}
to
\tilde{\Lambda}_{s}
satisfying

\displaystyle\Lambda_{N_{s}^{29}}\subset\tilde{\Lambda}_{s}\subset\Lambda_{N_{s}^{29}+50N_{s}^{5}}

and

\displaystyle\tilde{\Lambda}_{s}\cap\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{s^{\prime}}\neq\emptyset\Rightarrow\tilde{\Omega}_{\bm{k}}^{s^{\prime}}\subset\tilde{\Lambda}_{s}\ \text{for $s^{\prime}\leq s$ and $\bm{k}\in P_{s^{\prime}}$}.

From Lemma
8.1
, we can get a set
\mathscr{K}
with
m(\mathscr{K})=0
such that for
\theta\in\mathbb{T}\setminus\mathscr{K}
, there exists an integer
s(\theta)
such that
Q_{s}\cap\Lambda_{2N_{s}^{29}}=\emptyset
for
s\geq s_{0}(\theta)
. For
s\geq s_{0}(\theta)
, we have

\displaystyle\tilde{\Lambda}_{s}\cap Q_{s}\subset\Lambda_{2N_{s}^{29}}\cap Q_{s}=\emptyset,

which implies
\tilde{\Lambda}_{s}
is
s
-
{\rm good}
. Let
\tilde{\Lambda}_{s,0}=\Lambda_{\frac{1}{2}N_{s}^{29}}\cap\tilde{\Lambda}_{s}
. Recalling (
8.4
), one has for
\theta\in\mathbb{T}\setminus(\mathscr{K}\cup\mathscr{L})
,
s\geq s_{0}(\theta)
,

\displaystyle|F(\theta)|=|\xi_{\bm{0}}(\theta)|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\bm{n}^{\prime}\in\tilde{\Lambda}_{s},\bm{n}^{\prime\prime}\notin\tilde{\Lambda}_{s}}|\mathcal{T}_{\tilde{\Lambda}_{s}}^{-1}(E;\theta)(\bm{0},\bm{n}^{\prime})|\cdot|\phi(\bm{n}^{\prime}-\bm{n}^{\prime\prime})|\cdot|\xi_{\bm{n}^{\prime\prime}}(\theta)|

\displaystyle\leq(I)+(II),

where

\displaystyle(I)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\bm{n}^{\prime}\in\tilde{\Lambda}_{s,0},\bm{n}^{\prime\prime}\notin\tilde{\Lambda}_{s}}|\mathcal{T}_{\tilde{\Lambda}_{s}}^{-1}(E;\theta)(\bm{0},\bm{n}^{\prime})|\cdot|\phi(\bm{n}^{\prime}-\bm{n}^{\prime\prime})|\cdot|\xi_{\bm{n}^{\prime\prime}}(\theta)|,

\displaystyle(II)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\bm{n}^{\prime}\in\tilde{\Lambda}_{s}\setminus\tilde{\Lambda}_{s,0},\bm{n}^{\prime\prime}\notin\tilde{\Lambda}_{s}}|\mathcal{T}_{\tilde{\Lambda}_{s}}^{-1}(E;\theta)(\bm{0},\bm{n}^{\prime})|\cdot|\phi(\bm{n}^{\prime}-\bm{n}^{\prime\prime})|\cdot|\xi_{\bm{n}^{\prime\prime}}(\theta)|.

For
(I)
, we have by Theorem
1.1
, (
3.8
) and (
8.3
) that

\displaystyle(I)

\displaystyle\leq C(\theta,\alpha_{1},d)(N_{s}^{29})^{-\alpha_{1}+d}\|\mathcal{T}_{\tilde{\Lambda}_{s}}^{-1}\|_{d}\|\phi\|_{\alpha_{1}}

\displaystyle\leq C(\theta,\alpha_{1},d)\delta_{s}^{\frac{\alpha_{1}}{36}}\rightarrow 0\ \text{as $s\rightarrow\infty$}.

For
(II)
, we also have by Theorem
1.1
, (
3.8
) and (
8.3
) that

\displaystyle(II)

\displaystyle\leq C(\theta,\alpha_{1},d)(N_{s}^{29})^{-\alpha_{1}+d}\|\mathcal{T}_{\tilde{\Lambda}_{s}}^{-1}\|_{\alpha_{1}}\|\phi\|_{d}

\displaystyle\leq C(\theta,\alpha_{1},d)\delta_{s}^{\frac{\alpha_{1}}{36}}\rightarrow 0\ \text{as $s\rightarrow\infty$}.

This implies
F(\theta)=0
for
\theta\in\mathbb{T}\setminus(\mathscr{K}\cup\mathscr{L})
. Thus
\|F\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}=0
, which contradicts (
8.1
).

This proves Theorem
1.5
.
∎

The manuscript has no associated data.

Appendix A

Proofs concerning Remark
1.1
.

First, we verify functions in
Example 1
belong to class
\mathscr{V}.

We have

\displaystyle 2\leq\frac{|\sin\pi z|}{\|z\|_{\mathbb{T}}}\leq\pi.

By the continuity of
\frac{|\sin\pi z|}{\|z\|_{\mathbb{T}}}
and the compactness of
\mathbb{T}
, there is some
R>0
such that

\displaystyle 1\leq\frac{|\sin\pi z|}{\|z\|_{\mathbb{T}}}\leq 4,\ \forall z\in\mathbb{D}_{2R}.

Therefore, for
z,z^{\prime}\in\mathbb{D}_{R}
,

\displaystyle|\cos 2\pi z-\cos 2\pi z^{\prime}|

\displaystyle=2|\sin\pi(z+z^{\prime})|\cdot|\sin\pi(z-z^{\prime})|\geq 2\|z+z^{\prime}\|_{\mathbb{T}}\|z-z^{\prime}\|_{\mathbb{T}},

\displaystyle|\cos 2\pi z-\cos 2\pi z^{\prime}|

\displaystyle=2|\sin\pi(z+z^{\prime})|\cdot|\sin\pi(z-z^{\prime})|\leq 32\|z+z^{\prime}\|_{\mathbb{T}}\|z-z^{\prime}\|_{\mathbb{T}}.

For

\displaystyle v_{1}(z)=\cos 2\pi z+\lambda_{2}\cos^{2}2\pi z+\cdots+\lambda_{n}\cos^{n}2\pi z,

we have

\displaystyle v_{1}(z)-v_{1}(z^{\prime})=(\cos 2\pi z-\cos 2\pi z^{\prime})\left(1+\sum_{k=2}^{n}\lambda_{k}\left(\sum_{l=0}^{k-1}\cos^{l}2\pi z\cos^{k-1-l}2\pi z^{\prime}\right)\right).

Let

\displaystyle u_{1}(z,z^{\prime})=\sum_{k=2}^{n}\lambda_{k}\left(\sum_{l=0}^{k-1}\cos^{l}2\pi z\cos^{k-1-l}2\pi z^{\prime}\right).

From
\sup\limits_{z\in\mathbb{T}}|\cos 2\pi z|\leq 1
and
2|\lambda_{2}|+\cdots+n|\lambda_{n}|<1
, we get

\displaystyle\sup_{(z,z^{\prime})\in\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{T}}|u_{1}(z,z^{\prime})|\leq\sum_{k=2}^{n}k|\lambda_{k}|<1.

By the continuity of
u_{1}
and the compactness of
\mathbb{T}
, there is some
0<R_{1}<R
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{(z,z^{\prime})\in\mathbb{D}_{R_{1}}\times\mathbb{D}_{R_{1}}}|u_{1}(z,z^{\prime})|<1.

Hence, for
z,z^{\prime}\in\mathbb{D}_{R_{1}}
,

\displaystyle|v_{1}(z)-v_{1}(z^{\prime})|

\displaystyle\geq 2\left(1-\sup_{(z,z^{\prime})\in\mathbb{D}_{R_{1}}\times\mathbb{D}_{R_{1}}}|u_{1}(z,z^{\prime})|\right)\|z+z^{\prime}\|_{\mathbb{T}}\|z-z^{\prime}\|_{\mathbb{T}},

\displaystyle|v_{1}(z)-v_{1}(z^{\prime})|

\displaystyle\leq 64\|z+z^{\prime}\|_{\mathbb{T}}\|z-z^{\prime}\|_{\mathbb{T}},

which shows
v_{1}\in\mathscr{V}.

Next, we verify functions in
Example 2
belong to class
\mathscr{V}.
For

\displaystyle v_{2}(z)=\cos 2\pi z+\epsilon f(z),

we get

\displaystyle v_{2}(z)-v_{2}(z^{\prime})=(\cos 2\pi z-\cos 2\pi z^{\prime})\left(1+\epsilon\frac{f(z)-f(z^{\prime})}{\cos 2\pi z-\cos 2\pi z^{\prime}}\right).

Let

\displaystyle u_{2}(z,z^{\prime})=\frac{f(z)-f(z^{\prime})}{\cos 2\pi z-\cos 2\pi z^{\prime}}.

Since
f
is
1
-periodic and even, we have
f(z)=f(z+j)
and
f(z)=f(-z+j)
for
\forall j\in\mathbb{Z}
, which implies
u_{2}(z,z^{\prime})
only has removable singular points. Hence
u_{2}(z,z^{\prime})
can be continuous on
\mathbb{D}_{R}\times\mathbb{D}_{R}
and there exists some
C=C(f)>0
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{(z,z^{\prime})\in\mathbb{D}_{R}\times\mathbb{D}_{R}}\left|u_{2}(z,z^{\prime})\right|\leq C.

Therefore, for
z,z^{\prime}\in\mathbb{D}_{R}
, one has since
|\epsilon|C\leq\frac{1}{2},

\displaystyle\|z+z^{\prime}\|_{\mathbb{T}}\|z-z^{\prime}\|_{\mathbb{T}}\leq|v_{2}(z)-v_{2}(z^{\prime})|\leq 48\|z+z^{\prime}\|_{\mathbb{T}}\|z-z^{\prime}\|_{\mathbb{T}},

which implies
v_{2}\in\mathscr{V}
for
0<|\epsilon|\ll 1.

∎

The following elementary inequality plays an important role in the proof of our tame estimate.

Lemma A.1
.

Let
K(n,\alpha)
be given by (
3.2
). For
\alpha\geq 0
,
x_{i}\geq 0
and
1\leq i\leq n
, we have

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}x_{i}\right)^{\alpha}\leq K(n,\alpha)\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}x_{i}^{\alpha}\right).

(A.1)

Proof of Lemma
A.1
.

The proof of case
0\leq\alpha\leq 1
is trivial and we omit the details.

In the following, we only consider the case
\alpha>1.
We proceed by induction on
n.

The lemma holds trivially for
n=1
. Now, we assume (
A.1
) holds true for
n=m
and we will prove it for
n=m+1
. When
n=m+1
, we define for
\alpha>1
the function

\displaystyle f_{\alpha}(x_{1},\cdots,x_{m},x_{m+1})=\left(\sum_{i=1}^{m+1}x_{i}\right)^{\alpha}-K(m+1,\alpha)\left(\sum_{i=1}^{m+1}x_{i}^{\alpha}\right),\ x_{i}\geq 0,\ 1\leq i\leq m+1.

We have

\displaystyle\partial_{x_{m+1}}f_{\alpha}(x_{1},\cdots,x_{m},x_{m+1})=\alpha\left(\sum_{i=1}^{m+1}x_{i}\right)^{\alpha-1}-\alpha K(m+1,\alpha)x_{m+1}^{\alpha-1}.

Given
x_{i}^{0}\geq 0
(
1\leq i\leq m
), we have

\displaystyle\partial_{x_{m+1}}f_{\alpha}(x_{1}^{0},\cdots,x_{m}^{0},x_{m+1})=\alpha\left(\left(\sum_{i=1}^{m}x_{i}^{0}+x_{m+1}\right)^{\alpha-1}-((m+1)x_{m+1})^{\alpha-1}\right).

Since
x^{\alpha-1}
(
\alpha>1
) is non-decreasing on
[0,+\infty)
, we obtain

\displaystyle\partial_{x_{m+1}}f_{\alpha}(x_{1}^{0},\cdots,x_{m}^{0},x_{m+1})

\displaystyle\geq 0,\ 0\leq x_{m+1}\leq\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{m}x_{i}^{0}}{m},

\displaystyle\partial_{x_{m+1}}f_{\alpha}(x_{1}^{0},\cdots,x_{m}^{0},x_{m+1})

\displaystyle\leq 0,\ x_{m+1}>\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{m}x_{i}^{0}}{m}.

As a result, and since (
A.1
) holds for
n=m
, we get for all
x_{m+1}\geq 0,

\displaystyle f_{\alpha}(x_{1}^{0},\cdots,x_{m}^{0},x_{m+1})

\displaystyle\leq f_{\alpha}\left(x_{1}^{0},\cdots,x_{m}^{0},\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{m}x_{i}^{0}}{m}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{m+1}{m}\right)^{\alpha-1}\left(\left(\sum_{i=1}^{m}x_{i}^{0}\right)^{\alpha}-K(m,\alpha)\sum_{i=1}^{m}(x_{i}^{0})^{\alpha}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 0.

Therefore, for
\forall x_{i}\in[0,+\infty)
,
1\leq i\leq m+1
, we have

\displaystyle f_{\alpha}(x_{1},\cdots,x_{m},x_{m+1})\leq\sup_{x_{i}^{0}\geq 0\atop 1\leq i\leq m}f_{\alpha}(x_{1}^{0},\cdots,x_{m}^{0},x_{m+1})\leq 0,

which proves (
A.1
) for
n=m+1.

We thus complete the proof.

∎

Proof of Lemma
3.1
.

Using (
A.1
) yields for any
\bm{l},\bm{k}_{1},\cdots,\bm{k}_{n-1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d},

\displaystyle(1+\|\bm{l}\|)^{\alpha}

\displaystyle\leq K(n,\alpha)\left((1+\|\bm{l}-\bm{k}_{1}\|)^{\alpha}+(1+\|\bm{k}_{1}-\bm{k}_{2}\|)^{\alpha}+\cdots+(1+\|\bm{k}_{n-1}\|)^{\alpha}\right).

Then from the definition (
1.5
), we get

\displaystyle\|\prod_{i=1}^{n}\mathcal{M}_{i}\|_{\alpha}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\bm{l}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\left(\sup_{\bm{k}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\left|\left(\prod_{i=1}^{n}\mathcal{M}_{i}\right)(\bm{k}+\bm{l},\bm{k})\right|\right)(1+\|\bm{l}\|)^{\alpha}

\displaystyle\leq K(n,\alpha)\sum_{\bm{k}_{n-1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\cdots\sum_{\bm{k}_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\sum_{\bm{k}_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\sum_{\bm{l}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\left(\sup_{\bm{k}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|\mathcal{M}_{1}(\bm{k}+\bm{l},\bm{k}+\bm{k}_{1})|\right)

\displaystyle\ \ \cdot\left(\sup_{\bm{k}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|\mathcal{M}_{2}(\bm{k}+\bm{k}_{1},\bm{k}+\bm{k}_{2})|\right)\cdots\left(\sup_{\bm{k}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|\mathcal{M}_{n}(\bm{k}+\bm{k}_{n-1},\bm{k})|\right)

\displaystyle\ \ \times\left((1+\|\bm{l}-\bm{k}_{1}\|)^{\alpha}+(1+\|\bm{k}_{1}-\bm{k}_{2}\|)^{\alpha}+\cdots+(1+\|\bm{k}_{n-1}\|)^{\alpha}\right)

\displaystyle\leq K(n,\alpha)\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\prod_{j\neq i}\|\mathcal{M}_{j}\|_{0}\right)\|\mathcal{M}_{i}\|_{\alpha}.

∎

Proof of Lemma
3.6
.

By Hadamard’s inequality, we have for any
\bm{i},\bm{j}\in\Lambda,

\displaystyle|\langle\delta_{\bm{i}},\mathcal{S}_{\Lambda}^{*}\delta_{\bm{j}}\rangle|

\displaystyle\leq\prod_{\bm{l}\neq\bm{i}}\left(\sum_{\bm{k}\neq\bm{j}}|\langle\delta_{\bm{l}},\mathcal{S}_{\Lambda}\delta_{\bm{k}}\rangle|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\prod_{\bm{l}\neq\bm{i}}\left(\sum_{\bm{k}\neq\bm{j}}|\langle\delta_{\bm{l}},\mathcal{S}_{\Lambda}\delta_{\bm{k}}\rangle|\right)\ ({\rm since}\ \eqref{ee})

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathcal{S}_{\Lambda}\|_{0}^{\#\Lambda-1}.

Moreover, we have

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{S}_{\Lambda}^{*}\|_{0}\leq\sum_{\bm{i},\bm{j}\in\Lambda}|\langle\delta_{\bm{i}},\mathcal{S}_{\Lambda}^{*}\delta_{\bm{j}}\rangle|\leq(\#\Lambda)^{2}\|\mathcal{S}_{\Lambda}\|_{0}^{\#\Lambda-1}.

∎

Proof concerning Remark
4.1
.

Let
\bm{i}\in Q_{0}^{+}
and
\bm{j}\in\tilde{Q}_{0}^{-}
satisfy

\displaystyle\|\theta+\bm{i}\cdot\bm{\omega}+\theta_{0}\|_{\mathbb{T}}<\delta_{0},\ \|\theta+\bm{j}\cdot\bm{\omega}-\theta_{0}\|_{\mathbb{T}}<\delta_{0}^{\frac{2}{3}}.

Then (
1.4
) implies that
1,\omega_{1},\cdots,\omega_{d}
are rationally independent and
\{\bm{k}\cdot\bm{\omega}\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}
is dense in
\mathbb{T}
. Thus, there exist a
\bm{k}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
such that
\|2\theta+\bm{k}\cdot\bm{\omega}\|_{\mathbb{T}}
is sufficiently small with

\displaystyle\|\theta+(\bm{k}-\bm{j})\cdot\bm{\omega}+\theta_{0}\|_{\mathbb{T}}

\displaystyle\leq\|2\theta+\bm{k}\cdot\bm{\omega}\|_{\mathbb{T}}+\|\theta+\bm{j}\cdot\bm{\omega}-\theta_{0}\|_{\mathbb{T}}<\delta_{0}^{\frac{2}{3}},

\displaystyle\|\theta+(\bm{k}-\bm{i})\cdot\bm{\omega}-\theta_{0}\|_{\mathbb{T}}

\displaystyle\leq\|2\theta+\bm{k}\cdot\bm{\omega}\|_{\mathbb{T}}+\|\theta+\bm{i}\cdot\bm{\omega}+\theta_{0}\|_{\mathbb{T}}<\delta_{0}.

We obtain
\bm{k}-\bm{j}\in\tilde{Q}_{0}^{+}
and
\bm{k}-\bm{i}\in Q_{0}^{-}
, which implies

\displaystyle\text{dist}\left(\tilde{Q}_{0}^{+},Q_{0}^{-}\right)\leq\text{dist}\left(\tilde{Q}_{0}^{-},Q_{0}^{+}\right).

The similar argument shows

\displaystyle\text{dist}\left(\tilde{Q}_{0}^{+},Q_{0}^{-}\right)\geq\text{dist}\left(\tilde{Q}_{0}^{-},Q_{0}^{+}\right).

We have shown

\displaystyle\text{dist}\left(\tilde{Q}_{0}^{+},Q_{0}^{-}\right)=\text{dist}\left(\tilde{Q}_{0}^{-},Q_{0}^{+}\right).

∎

Lemma A.2

(Schur complement lemma)
.

Let
\Lambda_{1}
and
\Lambda_{2}
be finite subsets of
\mathbb{Z}^{d}
with
\Lambda_{1}\cap\Lambda_{2}=\emptyset
. Suppose
\mathcal{A}\in\mathbf{M}_{\Lambda_{1}}^{\Lambda_{1}}
,
\mathcal{B}\in\mathbf{M}_{\Lambda_{2}}^{\Lambda_{1}}
,
\mathcal{C}\in\mathbf{M}_{\Lambda_{1}}^{\Lambda_{2}}
,
\mathcal{D}\in\mathbf{M}_{\Lambda_{2}}^{\Lambda_{2}}
and

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\mathcal{A}&\mathcal{B}\\
\mathcal{C}&\mathcal{D}\end{array}\right)\in\mathbf{M}_{\Lambda_{1}\cup\Lambda_{2}}^{\Lambda_{1}\cup\Lambda_{2}}.

Assume further that
\mathcal{A}
is invertible and
\|\mathcal{B}\|_{0},\|\mathcal{C}\|_{0}\leq 1
. Then we have

(1)

\displaystyle\det\mathcal{M}=\det\mathcal{A}\cdot\det\mathcal{S},

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}=\mathcal{D}-\mathcal{C}\mathcal{A}^{-1}\mathcal{B}\in\mathbf{M}_{\Lambda_{2}}^{\Lambda_{2}}

is called the Schur complement of
\mathcal{A}
.

(2)

\mathcal{M}
is invertible iff
\mathcal{S}
is invertible and

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{S}^{-1}\|_{0}\leq\|\mathcal{M}^{-1}\|_{0}<4(1+\|\mathcal{A}^{-1}\|_{0})^{2}(1+\|\mathcal{S}^{-1}\|_{0}).

(A.2)

Proof of Lemma
A.2
.

(1) Since
\mathcal{A}
is invertible, we have

\displaystyle\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\mathcal{I}_{\Lambda_{1}}&\bm{0}\\
-\mathcal{C}\mathcal{A}^{-1}&\mathcal{I}_{\Lambda_{2}}\end{array}\right)\mathcal{M}\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\mathcal{I}_{\Lambda_{1}}&-\mathcal{A}^{-1}\mathcal{B}\\
\bm{0}&\mathcal{I}_{\Lambda_{2}}\end{array}\right)=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\mathcal{A}&\bm{0}\\
\bm{0}&\mathcal{S}\end{array}\right),

which implies

\displaystyle\det\mathcal{M}=\det\mathcal{A}\cdot\det\mathcal{S}.

(2) Direct computation shows

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}^{-1}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\mathcal{A}^{-1}+\mathcal{A}^{-1}\mathcal{B}\mathcal{S}^{-1}\mathcal{C}\mathcal{A}^{-1}&-\mathcal{A}^{-1}\mathcal{B}\mathcal{S}^{-1}\\
-\mathcal{S}^{-1}\mathcal{C}\mathcal{A}^{-1}&\mathcal{S}^{-1}\end{array}\right),

which combines (
3.1
) implying (
A.2
).
∎

Lemma A.3
.

Let
\bm{l}\in\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{Z}^{d}
and let
\Lambda\subset\mathbb{Z}^{d}+\bm{l}
be a finite set which is symmetrical about the origin (i.e.,
\bm{n}\in\Lambda\Leftrightarrow-\bm{n}\in\Lambda
). Then

\displaystyle\det\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda}(z)=\det((v(z+\bm{n}\cdot\bm{\omega})-E)\delta_{\bm{n},\bm{n}^{\prime}}+\varepsilon\mathcal{W})_{\bm{n}\in\Lambda}

is an even function of
z
.

Proof of Lemma
A.3
.

Define the unitary map

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}_{\Lambda}:\ell^{2}(\Lambda)\rightarrow\ell^{2}(\Lambda)\ \text{with}\ (\mathcal{U}_{\Lambda}\psi)(\bm{n})=\psi(-\bm{n}).

Then

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}_{\Lambda}^{-1}\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda}(z)\mathcal{U}_{\Lambda}=((v(z-\bm{n}\cdot\bm{\omega})-E)\delta_{\bm{n},\bm{n}^{\prime}}+\varepsilon\mathcal{W})_{n\in\Lambda}=\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda}(-z),

which implies

\displaystyle\det\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda}(z)=\det\mathcal{T}_{\Lambda}(-z).

∎

Lemma A.4
.

Let
\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B}:\ell^{2}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})\rightarrow\ell^{2}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})
be linear operators and let
\Lambda
be a finite subset of
\mathbb{Z}^{d}
. If
\|\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda}\|_{0}\leq M
and
\|\mathcal{B}_{\Lambda}\|_{0}\leq\varepsilon
, then

\displaystyle\left|\det(\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda}+\mathcal{B}_{\Lambda})-\det\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda}\right|\leq\varepsilon(\#\Lambda)^{2}(M+\varepsilon)^{\#\Lambda-1}.

(A.3)

Proof.

Let
f(t)=\det(\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda}+t\mathcal{B}_{\Lambda})
. Then

\displaystyle f^{\prime}(t)={\rm tr}(\mathcal{B}_{\Lambda}(\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda}+t\mathcal{B}_{\Lambda})^{*}).

Since
\|\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda}\|_{0}\leq M
and
|\mathcal{B}_{\Lambda}\|_{0}\leq\varepsilon
, we have

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda}+t\mathcal{B}_{\Lambda}\|_{0}\leq\|\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda}\|_{0}+|t|\cdot\|\mathcal{B}_{\Lambda}\|_{0}\leq M+\varepsilon|t|.

By Lemma
3.6
, we get for any
\bm{i},\bm{j}\in\Lambda
,

\displaystyle|\langle\delta_{\bm{i}},(\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda}+t\mathcal{B}_{\Lambda})^{*}\delta_{\bm{j}}\rangle|\leq\|\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda}+t\mathcal{B}_{\Lambda}\|_{0}^{\#\Lambda-1}\leq(M+\varepsilon|t|)^{\#\Lambda-1}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle|f^{\prime}(t)|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\bm{i}\in\Lambda}|\langle\delta_{\bm{i}},\mathcal{B}_{\Lambda}(\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda}+t\mathcal{B}_{\Lambda})^{*}\delta_{\bm{i}}\rangle|

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon(\#\Lambda)^{2}\max_{\bm{i},\bm{j}\in\Lambda}|\langle\delta_{\bm{i}},(\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda}+t\mathcal{B}_{\Lambda})^{*}\delta_{\bm{j}}\rangle|

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon(\#\Lambda)^{2}(M+\varepsilon|t|)^{\#\Lambda-1}.

According to the mean-value theorem, we obtain for some
\xi\in(0,1),

\displaystyle|\det(\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda}+\mathcal{B}_{\Lambda})-\det\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda}|=|f(1)-f(0)|=|f^{\prime}(\xi)|\leq\varepsilon(\#\Lambda)^{2}(M+\varepsilon)^{\#\Lambda-1}.

∎
